## Independent read findings and remaining qualification

The director's independent static read identified disconnected bad-map evidence, missing host feature isolation, a missing paging request, checksum-only malformed-data testing, PAT assumptions, unspecified framebuffer bytes, an overbroad assembly-necessity assertion, fabricated shared-vector diagnosis, references formed before RAM initialization, an overbroad static firmware slice, uncalibrated spin-based mask evidence and zero-high-half register patterns. Fixes use one production validator, a required freestanding feature, a real revision1 four-level paging request, actual parser corpora, every-point table rollback, PAT rejection/readback, full framebuffer bounds/padding, honest unknown-vector reporting, raw-before-reference initialization, narrow unsafe firmware borrows, actual PIT reload observations with a failing unmasked mutation, and full64-bit all-GPR probes. Investigating the compiler ABI removed the initial handwritten IRQ wrapper rather than rationalizing it. These findings/fixes are retained; formal architecture/memory/unsafe/security acceptance remains open, not inferred from author tests or this record.

The physical diagnostic payload is an experimental native-Limine ELF, now distributed in the separate unsigned ALPM archive above; neither artifact is a Linux-compatible system or replacement kernel package. A user-operated path is external Limine12.9.3 loading the separate diagnostic ELF from user-owned recoverable media with a32bpp framebuffer; it must not replace the current default entry/kernel. Systemd-boot cannot directly execute this ELF: native EFI/PE/Linux image work remains required. No physical install/boot/firmware/device write was performed. Before any workstation trial, independently review the exact image, confirm framebuffer/controller/firmware compatibility, user console and known-good manual recovery, and complete the candidate's QEMU suite. No serial hardware is assumed: only tty0 was observed, so framebuffer diagnostics are essential. Missing physical report/recovery authorization blocks physical qualification.

Still open: complete M1 CPU/platform/firmware/boot paths, calibrated clocks and physical results; SMP/NUMA/RT/hotplug, mature Linux-compatible allocation/accounting/reclaim and mappings; Linux process/ELF/syscall/signal/userspace semantics; every storage/filesystem/network/security/driver/architecture facility; full source-to-behavior registries, formal reviews, reproducibility/license release closure, safe packaging/recovery and matched pinned-Linux statistical performance. They remain mandatory, not waived or declared implemented by this cell. The baseline6.18.57 full-kernel port is **not complete**.

## Kernel services foundation

Branch `feat/kernel-foundation` (PR #13: services, heap and process/syscall commits, docs and review-fix commits on top of `main`). This is the base every later syscall, memory, VFS, task and signal feature builds on; it adds no syscall and changes nothing `/init` can observe. Evidence for the gate results is recorded under [Evidence](#kernel-services-foundation-evidence).

### What changed

| Area | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Boot state | `BootInfo`, `Allocator<'_>` and `Tables` were locals of `kernel_main`, whose frame is reused as init's kernel stack (`TSS.RSP0`) | `services.rs`: `BOOT` (write-once `Once<BootInfo>`, lock-free `&'static`), `FRAMES` (`Service<Allocator<'static>>`), `TABLES` (`Service<Tables>`), all behind the IRQ-saving `IrqMutex` built on `cpu::IrqSpinLock` (the only `cpu.rs` edit is its visibility, see below). Installed once by `publish_services`. |
| Table updates | "every table write precedes CR3 publication"; `user_space()` was `unsafe` and returned a copyable handle | The user half may be edited after activation (`paging.rs` module doc): `map_user`, `unmap_user`, `protect_user`, `map_user_anon`, `unmap_user_free` (later replaced by the `mm_*` operations, see [memory-mapping wiring](#memory-mapping-wiring)), with `invlpg`, atomic PTE read-modify-write and rollback. `UserSpace<'a>` now holds `&mut Tables`, so `user_space()` is safe and no edit can overlap a frame reference. |
| Frame splitting | arguments block was one order-2 reservation | `Allocator::split_reserved_order` (Linux `split_page`) makes its pages independently freeable; every user data frame is an order-0 reservation. |
| Heap | none | `kernel::heap::KernelHeap` (host-tested policy) over vendored `buddy_system_allocator` 0.13.0, growing from buddy frames; `kalloc.rs` is the `#[global_allocator]`. |
| Allocation API | n/a | kernel builds `alloc` with `--cfg=no_global_oom_handling`: `Box::new`, `Vec::push`, ... do not exist. |
| Process | `userspace::State { space, vmas }` behind a private spinlock | `userspace::Process { pid, tgid, mm, vmas, brk }` in `PROCESS: Service<Process>`; `syscall::dispatch(&mut impl Context, number, args)` |
| Syscall layout | `kernel/src/syscall.rs` | `syscall/mod.rs` (dispatch, `Outcome`, ENOSYS tables, `Context`/`Console`), `syscall/io.rs` (`write`), `syscall/process.rs` (`getpid`, `exit`, `exit_group`), `syscall/testing.rs` (host fakes) |

`cpu.rs` was touched in exactly four places and only by visibility: `IrqSpinLock` (struct, `new`, `lock`) and `IrqSpinGuard` became `pub(crate)`, plus the doc comment above them. No APIC/timer/IDT/entry code changed.

### Lock order and contexts

One DAG, documented in `services.rs`: `PROCESS` -> `TABLES` -> heap -> `FRAMES`; `FRAMES` is a leaf (it calls nothing that locks); nothing is held across a user copy that could fault (the kernel never touches user addresses), a callback or a blocking operation. All are `IrqMutex`: local interrupts are masked while held and restored after unlocking (`cpu.rs` guard order), so an interrupt handler can never deadlock against an interrupted holder on one CPU. NMI, `#DF` and `#MC` are not excluded and must never take these locks. **Allocation context: task context only.** The heap is deadlock-free if called with interrupts enabled, but there is no atomic reserve or GFP-style flag yet, so interrupt handlers MUST NOT allocate; the panic and fault paths only `log!`. The allocator never sleeps, reclaims, does I/O or uses reserves (`memory.rs`). Proof of the `Sync` arguments: `IrqMutex<T: Send>` (payload only reachable through a guard that holds the spinlock with IF masked); `Once<T: Send + Sync>` (single writer by CAS, Release store / Acquire load); `BootInfo: Send + Sync` (immutable data over reserved loader memory, no interior mutability); `Allocator: Send` (exclusive owner of its metadata, pointers are identities never dereferenced); `KernelHeap<S: Send>` is automatically `Send` because the vendored free list carries its own `unsafe impl Send` (the review found a redundant impl of ours, removed). Each has a `SAFETY` comment at the impl.

### Post-activation page-table rule

Kernel half: written only before `activate`, immutable after. User half: a not-present -> present change needs no invalidation (hardware caches no absent entry; SDM Vol. 3 4.10.4.3) and a new intermediate table is zeroed before it is linked; clearing or tightening a present PTE is an atomic RMW (hardware sets A/D bits concurrently) followed by `invlpg`, and only then may the frame be reused or freed. Empty intermediate user tables are not freed (no teardown exists; `shortcut:` in `paging.rs`). **SMP needs a TLB shootdown in place of the local `invlpg`, acknowledged before the frame is freed** (documented at the call sites; not built: one CPU). The `usercopy` TOCTOU contract is now true by construction: resolution and copy happen under one `&mut Tables` borrow, so unmap/protect cannot run between them.

### Fallible heap

Candidates read, the decision and the admitted configuration are in [dependencies](dependencies.md#kernel-heap-buddy_system_allocator): `talc` is rejected because its mandatory `allocator-api2` does not compile under `no_global_oom_handling`; `linked_list_allocator` cannot grow by discontiguous chunks; `slabmalloc` needs a pager plus a second allocator for large objects; `buddy_system_allocator` is admitted (MIT, no dependencies with default features off). Policy in `kernel/src/heap.rs`: growth by whole frame chunks (>= 64 KiB, up to the 4 MiB frame cap, Linux `KMALLOC_MAX_SIZE` analogue; `ORDERS` is 23 so the heap's largest block equals the frame cap), `None` for every failure (oversized, source refusal), no panic on any layout, power-of-two rounding (<= 50% internal fragmentation), chunks never returned (`shortcut:`). Probe evidence (rustc 1.101.0-nightly 5c543b0b8, scratch crate outside the repository, flags as the maintained build): `Vec::try_reserve`, `Vec::push_within_capacity`, `Box::try_new` compile, `Box::new` and `Vec::push` fail with `E0599`. Wired through `xtask` (`Target::build_std`, `remap_flags` appends `--cfg=no_global_oom_handling` for the kernel only, runtime archives require `alloc` for the kernel and none for the init), the package reproduction text, [kernel tooling](kernel-tooling.md#fallible-only-kernel-allocation) and `target/gates.sh` (outside git: the target Clippy line gained `env CARGO_ENCODED_RUSTFLAGS=--cfg=no_global_oom_handling` and `-Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins`; the previous script is `target/gates.sh.before-foundation`).

### Executable records

Two new `qemu-self-test` records, required by `SUCCESS_MARKERS` and `validate_success_serial` (exact, unique) with rejecting unit tests: `CHECK user-mm ok pages=3 rollback_points=3 tlb=invlpg protect=ro unmap=3 conflict=rolled-back accounting=restored` (rollback at each of three allocation points, copies through a fresh mapping, a write fault after `protect_user` and a read fault after unmap that a stale TLB entry would hide, conflict rollback; runs inside init's existing stack page table, so `CHECK userspace-audit` counts are unchanged) and `CHECK heap ok vec=1000 box=4096 align=4096 growth=buddy-frames oom=failed-cleanly recovered=true over_cap=rejected accounting=restored` (injected frame exhaustion through the allocator budget: the 4 MiB request fails, heap and frame accounting are unchanged, the same request then succeeds).

### The five design questions

1. *Crate?* Heap engine: yes, `buddy_system_allocator` (above, with three rejections); `lock_api`/`spin` rejected (they neither mask IRQs nor fit the lock order); `x86_64::instructions::tlb::flush` is the existing `invlpg` wrapper, so no new `asm!`.
2. *Bundle?* One allocator (`Allocator`), one lock type (`IrqMutex`), one user-copy path (`usercopy`), one page-table owner (`Tables`); the heap sits on the frame allocator instead of a second pool, and `UserSpace` is a borrow of `Tables` instead of a copied root.
3. *Simplify?* Removed the private `Shared`/`RawSpinLock` state cell and the unsafe `user_space`; the syscall context replaces the `UserPages` + `Console` pair. Not simplified: SMP shootdown, page-table freeing, chunk return, slab layer are named shortcuts, not hidden.
4. *Secure?* Assets: frames, page tables, the kernel heap. Attacker input reaches `unmap/protect` only through future syscalls; those paths validate alignment and `TASK_SIZE_MAX`, never touch the kernel half, check the user bit before reclaiming, and cannot free a frame twice (the allocator rejects non-live heads). Exhaustion paths are tested (budget injection) and leave no state change. Stale-TLB use-after-free is closed locally by `invlpg` before reuse (tested) and is an explicit SMP blocker. Heap contents of freed blocks are not zeroed (free-list pointers live in them); acceptable for kernel-internal data, revisit before heap-allocated user-visible buffers. Independent unsafe/architecture/security review has **not** occurred.
5. *Improve?* Measure and, if hot, add a slab front end for small fixed sizes and return idle chunks; replace the O(n) buddy scan; per-CPU caches with SMP. No measurement exists yet; none is claimed.

### Definition of done for this change

Persistent services (BOOT/FRAMES/TABLES statics, safe `user_space`) - `publish_services`, 18-case suite. Post-activation map/unmap/protect with rollback, `invlpg`, frame return - `CHECK user-mm`. Fallible heap and compile-time absence of infallible APIs - `CHECK heap`, xtask unit tests, probe evidence. Process context and syscall split - host tests identical in assertions, 18 cases unchanged (`-- mmap` still reports `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=9`). Not done: SMP shootdown, page-table freeing, chunk return, GFP-style atomic allocation, any new syscall, IRQ-context allocation, review.

### Review follow-up (PR #13)

* **Heap growth under `FRAMES` is loud.** The grow path takes `FRAMES` with `try_lock`; if it is held it panics (`FAIL panic ... lock-order violation`, then the boot stops). Chosen over spinning (silent hang, the reviewed defect) and over returning an allocation failure (it would hide a kernel bug as `ENOMEM` to the user). It is correct for this cell because there is one CPU and every critical section masks interrupts, so a held `FRAMES` at that point can only be the allocating caller itself. With SMP the owner may be another CPU and the check must become "owner is this CPU" (`shortcut:` in `services.rs`). The rule is stated in `services.rs`, `kalloc.rs`, `Tables::mm_map_anon` and `Process::vmas`: reserve heap memory first, then take `FRAMES`. Not covered by a QEMU case (a positive control would deliberately stop the boot).
* **Negative control for `invlpg`, observed under QEMU TCG** (the `qemu-self-test` build, otherwise this branch's source): replacing the `tlb::flush` in both `unmap_user` and `protect_user` by a no-op made `kernel-test` match 7/18 cases with the success case ending `FAIL user-mm error=Conflict` (run `mut-noflush`); removing only the `unmap_user` flush (`mut-line414`) and only the `protect_user` flush (`mut-line461`) each failed the same way (7/18). So TCG does model the stale translation here and `CHECK user-mm` detects either omission. This is evidence about TCG's TLB model, not about hardware (the same code is expected to need the flush on real CPUs, which have larger TLBs and paging-structure caches); the run directories are untracked under `target/kernel-tooling/`, and the mutated source was restored (`git status` clean).
* **Heap block cap** is now 4 MiB (`ORDERS` 23) equal to the frame cap; the self-test rejects 8 MiB and 16 MiB requests before the source is asked. `KernelHeap`'s redundant `unsafe impl Send` was removed. `unmap_user` checks the user bit before clearing the PTE. The heap lock is held with interrupts masked during chunk zeroing (up to 4 MiB) and free-list scans: IRQ latency consequence documented in `kalloc.rs`.
* **Tests of `IrqMutex`/`Once`/`Service`** remain QEMU-covered only (they live in the binary); moving them into the library over a lock trait for host tests is a recorded improvement, not done.

### Kernel services foundation evidence

Run on the author's workstation (same host, user and toolchain installation as earlier records; not independently attested), `target/gates.sh` with the updated target Clippy line: run `fgate1` before the PR review and `fgate2` (logs `target/gates-foundation2/`, untracked) after the review fixes. Both: fmt, host 1.88 Clippy and tests (xtask 72, baremetal 10), exact-nightly workspace Clippy and tests (kernel 128 unit tests, i.e. the earlier 121 plus 5 heap-policy tests, 1 split-reserved test and 1 getpid-identity test; xtask 72; baremetal 10; 2 doc tests), `kernel-test` **18/18** matched (`target/kernel-tooling/fgate2`; the `-- mmap` case still ends `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=9`; `CHECK user-mm` and `CHECK heap` present in the success case), target Clippy for both kernel profiles with `--cfg=no_global_oom_handling` and build-std `alloc` (clean under `-D warnings`), `xtask check` (23 vendored packages verified) and `status`, all `exit=0`. Not run: physical hardware, SMP, performance comparison, independent review of the follow-up commit.

## Framebuffer console on real hardware

Scope: the target workstation has two GPUs (monitors on the discrete card, iGPU outputs disconnected), no serial port, and a long test log must be photographed from the screen. Four defects of the earlier console are repaired; nothing here is physical evidence (QEMU only, below).

| Defect | Change |
| --- | --- |
| Only Limine framebuffer 0 used (may be the disconnected iGPU) | `BootInfo::take_framebuffers` (`kernel/src/boot.rs`) reads every entry Limine reports, at most `MAX_FRAMEBUFFERS` = 8, each through the previous per-entry checks (pointer slot and struct inside bootloader-reclaimable memory, `pitch*height` extent inside a kind-7 region); a bad entry is `None` and the others are kept. `console::init` validates each survivor again with `fbtext::validate` and draws every character on all accepted ones. |
| Only 32 bpp 8/8/8 accepted (a 10-bit GOP mode left a blank screen) | `fbtext::validate` accepts RGB model, 16/24/32 bpp, any non-empty, non-overlapping channel sizes/shifts inside the pixel (A2R10G10B10, RGB565, XRGB1555, 24-bit packed, ...). Foreground is every colour bit set; pixels are written as an aligned `u16`/`u32` store or three byte stores. `validate` also requires `address + pitch*height` not to wrap, so `put`'s bound argument is local. Rejects (model, depth, size, pitch, alignment, channels, overlap) are printed per framebuffer on the screens that work. **If every framebuffer is rejected nothing can be drawn: the screen stays blank, exactly as before this change, and the reasons reach only COM1 in the `qemu-self-test` profile (the default/physical profile has no other sink). That case is therefore still indistinguishable from a kernel that never started; the audit's A3 blank-screen risk is reduced to "no entry drawable", not removed.** |
| Output wrapped to the top and overwrote itself | The cursor passes the last row lazily: a character or newline at the pending position scrolls once. The last line is therefore not wasted and a trailing newline does not scroll. The wrap is deferred like Linux's `vc_need_wrap`: a line of exactly `cols` characters followed by `\n` produces no blank line (cursor state `pos<<1 | wrap`, `fbtext::step`). `\t` advances to the next multiple of 8 columns with spaces as fbcon does; other control bytes still draw `?`, and `\r` is ignored. |
| 8x16 is tiny on a 2560x1440 panel | Glyph scale follows Linux's `get_default_font` (below). |

**Provenance.** Font choice: Linux 6.18.57 `lib/fonts/fonts.c:111-151` (`get_default_font`, scoring loop reproduced in `fbtext::choose_scale`), candidate `pref` values from `font_8x16.c:4632` (VGA8x16, 0), `font_8x8.c:2583` (VGA8x8, 0) and `font_ter16x32.c:2068` (TER16x32, -1 off SPARC), call site `drivers/video/fbdev/core/fbcon.c:995-998`. We keep only `font8x8` 0.3.1 (already admitted, glyph data only); 8x8 is scale 1x1, VGA8x16 is the existing 1x2 line doubling, TER16x32 is 2x4. Scales 3x6 and 4x8 reuse TER16x32's `pref` and win only where Linux's formula already prefers more than 16x32 and Linux has no font (an extension, never selected below 4K-class panels). Results: 640x350 -> 8x8, 1024x768 and 1920x1080 -> 8x16, 2560x1440 and 3840x2160 -> 16x32 (as the pinned scoring picks TER16x32 there), 7680x4320 -> 32x64. No new crate and no new font data. The scoring assumes no blit mask (`font_w`/`font_h` NULL adds 1000 to every font); Linux passes `info->pixmap.blit_x/blit_y` at `fbcon.c:995-998`, so a driver that restricts blit widths could make real Linux choose differently. Not reproduced: Linux's actual TER16x32 and VGA8x16 glyph shapes (the scaled 8x8 glyphs look coarser), fbcon's other fonts and the `fbcon=font:` selector.

**Scrolling: shadow redraw, not framebuffer copy.** A static `SHADOW` (`MAX_COLS` 256 x `MAX_ROWS` 128 glyph indices, 32 KiB BSS; atomics, so no reference is ever formed to it) holds the text. A scroll shifts the shadow up one row, zeroes the last row and redraws only the cells whose glyph changed (old vs new shadow entry, a never-written `0` counting as a space), scanline-major within each row so adjacent changed cells are written in ascending runs. Reason: framebuffers are normally write-combined (`paging.rs` maps kind 7 as PAT[5] WC) and reads from WC/UC are far slower than writes; a copy within the framebuffer would read ~15 MB per scrolled line at 1440p. A changed cell is rewritten completely (glyph and background), so a scrolled-in line cannot show stale pixels; unchanged cells already show the right pixels. Trade-off: because unchanged cells are not rewritten, a cell garbled by a reentrant or interleaved writer is no longer repaired by the next scroll, only by being written again (acceptable under the garble-only contract). Cost: writes proportional to changed cells (a log screen is mostly blank-over-blank), not the full `width*height*bytes` (about 15 MB at 2560x1440x4) per line - still unmeasured on hardware. `shortcut:` if a physical photo run is still too slow, scroll in batches. The initial clear writes the whole framebuffer black (removing the firmware logo). **Write-combining drain:** every `write_bytes` call and every scroll ends with `core::arch::x86_64::_mm_sfence()`, so the last lines before `cli; hlt` leave the WC buffers (Intel/AMD drain partial WC buffers at their discretion). It is an intrinsic, not `asm!`; `#[target_feature(enable = "sse")]` on our own function is a hard `x86_softfloat_sse` error on this target, so it is an out-of-line `unsafe` call to core's `_mm_sfence`, whose disassembly is exactly `sfence; ret` (`target/console-evidence/sfence-disassembly.txt`, from the freestanding ELF of run `console-005`). It touches no XMM/MMX state, flags, registers or stack; SFENCE exists on every x86_64 CPU. QEMU cannot show its effect; it is a real-hardware-only measure.

**Multiple framebuffers.** One logical grid shared by all: columns/rows are the minimum over accepted screens, each at its own scale, so a larger screen has an unused right/bottom margin. The `console: framebuffers used=...` line names the limiting framebuffer (`limit=fbN`); a small iGPU mode would shrink the grid on the 2560x1440 panel (e.g. 160 to 128 columns). A grid chosen from the largest screen was not taken because a screen with a smaller grid would then lose text. Slots are published with a single `COUNT` release store; the cursor is the only mutable coordination.

**Emergency/no-lock contract (restated, console.rs header).** No lock and no allocation; framebuffer bytes are only volatile exposed-provenance stores (readback only in the QEMU self-test); `fbtext::step` is the pure cursor transition and a compare-exchange hands each character a cell and decides who scrolls. Reentrant or interleaved writers (an exception during output) can garble text but cannot index outside the grid or the framebuffer (`put` re-checks x/y against width/height; `step` clamps out-of-range cursor state). Interleaving remains permitted, whole-line atomicity is not promised.

**Tests.** Host (`kernel` lib): 9 `fbtext` tests - standard, 10-bit, 565, 555 and 24-bit formats, every reject reason including a wrapping extent, the Linux font scores, grid clamping, lazy-scroll and deferred-wrap cursor steps, and a simulated log through a 4x3 grid - plus `boot::framebuffer_tests` for the per-entry loader logic (`gather_framebuffers`, memory access injected: count 0, count above the bound, null slot, out-of-memory framebuffer, overflowing extent, array outside loader memory). `take_framebuffers` itself reads the Limine request statics and is only the thin wrapper that supplies the real memory readers, so it runs only under QEMU. QEMU (`qemu-self-test` only): `console::self_test` writes `rows+2` six-character marker lines and a full-width line plus newline, and logs `CHECK console ok framebuffers=N scrolled=true tail=true stale=false readback=true wrap=true`: the shadow tail is the last `rows` lines, no cell beyond the marker is stale, the pixels of the top, middle and bottom rows of every framebuffer (read back through MMIO) equal what the shadow glyphs must render (this is what proves the changed-cell scroll), and the full line did not produce a blank line. `xtask kernel-test` requires exactly one such record, before `CHECKPOINT`, with `framebuffers=2` in the `success` case (its QEMU also attaches `-device ramfb`, giving OVMF a second GOP handle) and `framebuffers=1` in the other seventeen; a unit test rejects a wrong count or a failed field. The case catalog size is unchanged (18).

**QEMU evidence** (not physical; the paths below are local, untracked and disappear with the worktree, so they cannot be re-verified from a clone; ad hoc runs of the same default `freestanding` kernel image from `target/kernel-tooling/console-002/default-boot`, screendumps through QMP, under `target/worktrees/console/target/console-evidence/`): `run1/` std VGA + ramfb, `console: framebuffers used=2 reported=2` and both screens drawn; `r480/vga.png` Limine `resolution: 640x480x32` - the 33-line log scrolls, the head is gone and the tail ("init: ok", the panic line) is intact, nothing overwritten; `qhd/vga.png` `2560x1440x32` on a 64 MiB VGA - 16x32 glyphs, grid 160x45. The ad hoc screendumps used the builds of runs `console-002` and `console-005` (the latter has the changed-cell scroll; its 640x480 dump is identical in layout to the full-redraw one). The final maintained `kernel-test` run on the rebased branch is `target/kernel-tooling/console-gates5` (18/18); the full gate summary is `target/gates-console5/summary.txt` (all `exit=0`; host 1.88 xtask 73 + baremetal 10, nightly kernel 138 + xtask 73 + baremetal 10 + 2 doc). QEMU/OVMF always delivers BGRX8888, so 10-bit, 565 and 24-bit are covered only by the host unit tests, not by an executed framebuffer; nor is a multi-resolution mix.

**Touched outside `console.rs`:** `kernel/src/boot.rs` (`take_framebuffer` replaced by `take_framebuffers`, `MAX_FRAMEBUFFERS`, `Framebuffers`), `kernel/src/main.rs` (the `console::init` call, one `console::self_test` call under `qemu-self-test`, a comment), `kernel/src/lib.rs` (`pub mod fbtext`), new `kernel/src/fbtext.rs`, `xtask/src/kernel.rs` (`DebugCase.framebuffers`, ramfb argument, console record check, tests).

**Five questions.** *Preexisting crate?* `font8x8` is kept; no crate offers Linux's font-selection rule or this cursor/scroll model, and a console crate would add its own allocation. *Bundle?* Format validation, font choice and cursor stepping share one pure module (`fbtext`) used by the console and tests, one drawing path (`put`) serves clear, cell and redraw. *Simplify?* A shadow plus whole redraw instead of copy/diff/damage tracking; one shared grid instead of per-screen cursors. *Secure?* All loader fields are untrusted: independent per-entry validation, checked arithmetic, aligned access widths, volatile MMIO only, bounded loops (8 framebuffers, 256x128 cells, <= 16384 pixels per side), no references into MMIO, no panics reachable from loader values (`validate` shifts only after bounding `shift+size <= bpp <= 32`); `step` clamps. A hostile extent is limited by the kind-7 check; the mapping itself is unchanged. *Improve?* Measure the scroll cost on the workstation; diff-redraw or batch scrolling; Linux's real TER16x32/VGA8x16 glyphs through a vetted font crate or a provenance-preserving port; hardware-accurate EDID/preferred-screen selection; an on-screen banner with the source/ELF hash.

## Memory-mapping model

Pure Rust library module `kernel/src/mm/` (`#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`, no hardware access, 35 host tests) that reproduces Linux 6.18.57's memory-mapping decisions for one single-threaded process. It is wired into the running kernel by the [wiring record](#memory-mapping-wiring) below (process state, loader, syscalls, page faults, kernel usercopy). Nothing here is a whole-kernel, performance or physical claim.

### What it reproduces

| File | Linux source (pinned 6.18.57) | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `flags.rs` | `include/linux/mm.h` (`VM_*`, `VM_STACK_FLAGS`), `mman.h`, uapi `mman*.h`, `mm/vma.h` | `VmFlags` newtype with the bits of the declared configuration, `PROT_*`/`MAP_*`, `calc_vm_*`, `is_{exec,stack,data}_mapping`, `page_prot` |
| `space.rs` | `mm/vma.c` (`__split_vma`, `vma_merge_new_range`, `vma_merge_existing_range`, `vma_modify`, `vms_gather/complete_munmap_vmas`, `anon_vma_compatible`, `find_mergeable_anon_vma`, `acct_stack_growth`, `expand_downwards`, `insert_vm_struct`), `mm/mmap.c` (`find_vma`, `may_expand_vm`) | `Mm`: sorted `Vec<Vma>` (first VMA with `end > addr`), `Vma` with `vm_pgoff`, file identity and `anon_vma` identity, merge/split/unmap primitives, statistics (`total_vm`, `data_vm`, `exec_vm`, `stack_vm`, `locked_vm`), stack growth |
| `layout.rs` | `arch/x86/mm/mmap.c` (`mmap_base`, `mmap_address_hint_valid`), `arch/x86/kernel/sys_x86_64.c` (`arch_get_unmapped_area{,_topdown}`), `mm/vma.c` (`unmapped_area{,_topdown}`), `mm/mmap.c` (`__get_unmapped_area`, `round_hint_to_min`), `cap_mmap_addr` | `norandmaps` layout bases, top-down and bottom-up search with the stack guard rules, hint handling, `MAP_FIXED`, `MAP_32BIT`, `MAP_ABOVE4G`, `TASK_SIZE` limits |
| `ops.rs` | `mm/mmap.c` (`do_mmap`, `ksys_mmap_pgoff`, `brk`, `vm_brk_flags`), `mm/vma.c` (`mmap_region`, `do_brk_flags`, `do_vmi_munmap`), `mm/mprotect.c`, `sys_x86_64.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE6(mmap)`, `mm/gup.c` populate flags | `Mm::mmap`/`mmap_region`/`munmap`/`mprotect`/`brk`/`vm_brk_flags`/`populate` with Linux's check order and errno |
| `fault.rs` | `arch/x86/mm/fault.c` (`handle_page_fault`, `do_user_addr_fault`, `access_error`), `mm/mmap_lock.c` `lock_mm_and_find_vma`, `mm/memory.c` (`do_anonymous_page`, `handle_pte_fault`, `do_wp_page`) | `Mm::handle_user_fault` -> `FaultOutcome` (`Handled`, `Segv{MapErr/AccErr}`, `Bus`, `Oom`, `PgtableBad`, `Vsyscall`, `NeedsFile`) |
| `pt.rs` | `mm/memory.c`, `mm/mprotect.c` `change_protection` | `UserPages` trait: `lookup`, `map_zero_page`, `map_anon_page`, `make_writable`, `unmap`, `protect` |
| `env.rs` | `mm/util.c` `__vm_enough_memory`, `INIT_RLIMITS`, `security/min_addr.c` | `Env`: rlimits, `vm.max_map_count` 65530, `stack_guard_gap`, `mmap_min_addr` 4096, overcommit mode 0/1, `CAP_IPC_LOCK`, `CAP_SYS_RAWIO`; `FileInfo` (what `do_mmap` reads from a file) |

Declared configuration (reference config unless noted): x86-64, 4-level paging, `norandmaps`, `sysctl_legacy_va_layout = 0` (top-down), `CONFIG_COMPAT_BRK=n`, `CONFIG_TRANSPARENT_HUGEPAGE=n`, `CONFIG_KSM=n`, `CONFIG_USERFAULTFD=n`, `CONFIG_MEM_SOFT_DIRTY=n` (so `VM_SOFTDIRTY` is 0 and merging has no ignored flags), no protection keys (`qemu64` has no OSPKE: `execute_only_pkey` is -1), no shadow stack, `CONFIG_STACK_GROWSUP=n`, 64-bit task without `READ_IMPLIES_EXEC`, `MAP_DROPPABLE` real (`CONFIG_64BIT`).

Behaviours worth knowing, each from the cited source: a non-fixed hint is rounded **down** and not checked against `mmap_min_addr` (x86 override); `anon_vma` identity decides merging (two separately written VMAs never merge, a new neighbour reuses one only through `find_mergeable_anon_vma`); `munmap` hole punching and `mprotect` splits are refused with `ENOMEM` at `map_count >= max_map_count`, `do_mmap` at `>`; a failed `mmap_region` limit check leaves the edge splits made by `__mmap_setup`; `mprotect` over a hole changes the leading VMAs then returns `ENOMEM`; the heap never merges into the bss VMA ending at `start_brk` (`vma_prev_limit`) but later growth extends the heap VMA; stack expansion runs before `access_error` and reserves `stack_guard_gap` below an accessible neighbour; a fetch from a readable non-executable page first demand-maps it and only the retried fault is `SEGV_ACCERR`.

### Not modelled (named, not hidden)

* `MAP_SHARED` anonymous memory (needs `shmem_zero_setup`) and `MAP_HUGETLB`/hugetlbfs files return `Mmap::Unsupported(..)` **before any change**; they are not emulated. Owners: shmem/tmpfs and hugetlb workstreams (WP-M3.6).
* File-backed mappings run every `do_mmap` check and return `Mmap::NeedsFile(FileMapping)` unchanged; the VFS owner calls `->mmap` and then `Mm::mmap_region`. Faults in file VMAs return `NeedsFile`. `memfd_check_seals_mmap`, `->get_unmapped_area` file operations, `->mmap_prepare` and `->close` hooks are absent.
* `fork`/`vma_is_fork_child`/COW sharing, `mremap`, `madvise`, `mlock*`, `mseal`, `userfaultfd`, MDWE, personality flags, `OVERCOMMIT_NEVER` (needs `vm_committed_as`; `Overcommit` has no such variant), commit accounting (`vm_acct_memory` is not counted), NUMA policy, memcg, `MAP_POPULATE` of file pages, prefetch/erratum fix-ups.
* **Vsyscall page.** The reference configuration has `CONFIG_LEGACY_VSYSCALL_XONLY=y`: Linux *emulates* a user fetch at `0xffffffffff600000` (`emulate_vsyscall`). Not implemented; the fault is the distinct loud `FaultOutcome::Vsyscall` (nothing changed) so the wiring step stops or implements the emulation. A write or data read there is refused by `emulate_vsyscall` itself and is `SEGV_MAPERR`, as modelled (`PF_INSTR` stands in for `address == regs->ip`).
* `Vec::try_reserve` failure cannot be injected on the host; the failure atomicity argument is by construction (each primitive reserves before it mutates). The kernel-heap OOM path of this module is therefore unexercised under QEMU until it is wired.
* Locks, RCU and the per-VMA lock: one task, the caller holds the address-space lock. SMP needs a new synchronisation design (`mmap_lock`, per-VMA locks), not a port of these methods.

### Page-table trait: what the wiring step had to provide (done, see [Memory-mapping wiring](#memory-mapping-wiring))

`UserPages` maps onto the page-table operations (first the foundation's `Tables::{map_user_anon, unmap_user(_free), protect_user}`, since replaced by the `mm_*` family of the wiring) with these additions: a shared read-only zero frame (`map_zero_page`; do not allocate a private frame instead, RSS and `mincore` would differ); a `PAGE_NONE` representation that keeps the frame (`protect` with `accessible == false`); `map_anon_page` replacing a zero-page PTE; `protect` must never raise the hardware write bit of a private page (the write fault does through `make_writable`). Lock rule (foundation): reserve heap memory (`Mm` does `try_reserve` inside every primitive, before touching the tables) **before** taking `FRAMES`; do not call `Mm` methods while holding `FRAMES`. Library build: `extern crate alloc` and `#![feature(vec_push_within_capacity)]` are enabled in `kernel/src/lib.rs`; under `--cfg no_global_oom_handling` only `try_reserve`, `push_within_capacity`, `remove`, `drain` and `rotate_right` are used on `Vec`.

One mechanism: `kernel/src/exec/plan.rs` carried its own `do_mmap`/`__get_unmapped_area`/`vm_brk_flags` argument checks for `PT_LOAD`; they are **deleted** and `exec/load.rs` calls `Mm` (one mapping-rule mechanism, not two).

Loader contract: `Mm::insert_vm_struct` for the stack (`VM_STACK_FLAGS`, `pgoff = start >> 12`; unlike Linux's `create_init_stack_vma` it accounts `total_vm`/`stack_vm` itself, so the counters cannot disagree with the VMA set), `Mm::mmap_region` with the file identity for `PT_LOAD`, `Mm::vm_brk_flags` for `.bss`, and `start_brk`, `brk`, `start_data`, `end_data` set from the load plan.

### Evidence

Host tests (`cargo +nightly test -p kernel --lib mm::`, 37 tests): the placement, `MAP_FIXED`/`MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE`, argument-error, `munmap`, `mprotect` and `brk` expectations are transcribed from the oracle test source [`user/init/src/tests/mm.rs`](../user/init/src/tests/mm.rs) (merged with PR #15; test comments name the check), whose 121 `mm` checks passed on the pinned reference kernel under `vdso=0 norandmaps` (`xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`; local capture `target/linux-reference/init-reference-006`, whose logs hold only `ok` lines, so the values are in the test source, not the logs), and from hand derivation of the cited source for everything the oracle does not cover (named `src` in each test). A randomized sequence test checks the VMA invariants, statistics and that no PTE is outside a VMA or more permissive than it. There is no differential run of this module against Linux beyond these ported oracle values.

### Gate run

`target/gates.sh` run `mmgate4` on the author's workstation (same host, user and toolchain installation as earlier records; not independently attested), tree = branch `feat/mm-model` rebased on `main` cdd263d: `cargo fmt --all --check`, host 1.88 Clippy and tests of `xtask`/`baremetal`, nightly workspace Clippy (`-D warnings`) and tests (kernel library **175** passed, of which 37 are `mm::`), `xtask kernel-test` **18/18** cases matched (unchanged: the module is not linked into any case), both target Clippy profiles (`qemu-self-test`, `freestanding`) under `-Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins --cfg=no_global_oom_handling`, `xtask check` and `xtask status` (17 `in_progress`, 2,606 `not_started`) all `exit=0` (logs `target/gates-mm4/`, untracked). An earlier run (`mmgate3`) failed Clippy on a cast and an `assert!(is_empty())` introduced by the review fixes; they were fixed and the gates re-run in full. Review-fix changes after the first PR review: routed rows, `IS_ERR_VALUE` addresses from `MAP_FIXED` returned as errno, the distinct `FaultOutcome::Vsyscall`, 64-bit `anon_vma` identities (no wrap), `insert_vm_struct` accounting statistics itself with saturating decrements, oracle-source provenance wording.

### The five design questions

1. *Crate?* None. `bitflags` is vendored, but a 20-line newtype with the exact Linux bit values is simpler than a macro dependency here; `rangemap`/`iset`/`btree-slab`/`slab` were rejected in the crate research (compile failure under `no_global_oom_handling` or semantic mismatch); the intrusive RB tree needs a non-`forbid(unsafe_code)` scope and has no gap augmentation, so a sorted `Vec` (O(log n) lookup, O(n) insert) is the first version.
2. *Bundle?* One address-space model for `mmap`/`munmap`/`mprotect`/`brk`/fault/stack growth/loader, sharing one set of split, merge and unmap primitives, as Linux does in `mm/vma.c`; one page-table trait for the paging owner, no second allocator or copy layer.
3. *Simplify?* The maple tree, `vma_iterator`, `vm_area_struct` hooks, `anon_vma` object graph and locking are replaced by a sorted vector and an identity (only equality is observable). Not simplified: check order, `max_map_count` strictness, `anon_vma` merge rules and the leftover splits, because each is observable.
4. *Secure?* No `unsafe`; every address arithmetic uses checked, saturating or explicitly wrapping operations that mirror Linux's `unsigned long` behaviour (`PAGE_ALIGN` wrap, `start + len` wrap); no user pointer is dereferenced (the module never sees user memory); allocation is fallible (`ENOMEM`, no panic path on user input except `debug_assert!`s that state internal invariants); a VMA count budget (`vm.max_map_count`) bounds the vector; a PTE is never created outside a VMA and never writable without `VM_WRITE` (property test).
5. *Improve?* Replace the vector by an augmented tree when `map_count` makes O(n) insert measurable (Linux's default limit is 65530, so the worst case is bounded); add `mremap`/`madvise`/`mlock`, shmem, fork-time `anon_vma` chains, `OVERCOMMIT_NEVER`; run the oracle `mm` group against the wired kernel (`kernel-test`) and extend it with the cases only `src`-derived here (stack growth limits, guard gap, split/merge counts through `/proc/self/maps` once procfs exists).

## Memory-mapping wiring

Roadmap slices: WP-M3.1 (VMA/`mmap` family, demand paging) and the user-memory part of WP-M1.5. The [model](#memory-mapping-model) is now the only mapping-rule mechanism of the running kernel. Still one task, one CPU, anonymous memory only; nothing here is a whole-kernel, performance or physical claim.

### What is wired

| Path | Change |
| --- | --- |
| [`kernel/src/userspace.rs`](../kernel/src/userspace.rs) | `Process` owns `space: kernel::mm::Mm` and `env: Env` (replacing the fixed `Vmas` array and `brk`). `load_init` builds the address space through the model (below). `Syscall::page` (the one `usercopy::UserPages`) faults non-resident pages in. `exception` sends a ring-3 #PF to `page_fault` (`Mm::handle_user_fault`) and returns to user on `Handled`. `check_mm_usercopy` (self-test builds) |
| [`kernel/src/exec/load.rs`](../kernel/src/exec/load.rs) (library, host-tested) | `load_elf_binary`'s mapping half: `insert_stack` (`create_init_stack_vma`/`setup_arg_pages`: `Mm::insert_vm_struct`, `VM_STACK_FLAGS`), `map_segments` (`elf_load`: `Mm::mmap` with `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` for the first and `MAP_FIXED` for later segments, file identity `1`, then `Mm::mmap_region`; `Mm::vm_brk_flags` for the bss; the post-mapping `TASK_SIZE` checks; `start_brk`/`brk`/`start_data`/`end_data`), and the `Pager` trait (the page-cache stand-in: install each file page as a private frame right after its mapping is committed, so a later `MAP_FIXED` segment frees what it replaces) |
| [`kernel/src/exec/plan.rs`](../kernel/src/exec/plan.rs) | Only the binfmt side stays (header/program-table acceptance, `elf_load` arithmetic, page contents); its `do_mmap`/`__get_unmapped_area`/`vm_brk_flags` argument checks, `Vmas` and overlap refusal are **deleted**. `Unsupported::Overlap` now means only "a later `MAP_FIXED` segment replaced the argument stack" |
| [`kernel/src/exec/report.rs`](../kernel/src/exec/report.rs) | `FileRegion::of(&Mm, address)` is `print_vma_addr`'s `vma_lookup` with `vm_file`; the segfault and trap lines take it instead of the deleted `Vmas` |
| [`kernel/src/paging.rs`](../kernel/src/paging.rs) | `Tables::{init_zero_page, mm_lookup, mm_map_zero, mm_map_anon, mm_install_page, mm_make_writable, mm_unmap, mm_protect}` and the `kernel::mm::UserPages` impl for `UserSpace` (`FRAMES` locked per operation; the loader's `LoadSpace` is gone, see the VFS and memory-mapping integration section). `PROT_NONE` is a not-present user PTE with software bit 8 (`_PAGE_PROTNONE`) and the frame kept **inverted** (Linux's L1TF guard, below); every "mapped" walker (including the isolation audit) tests `MAPPED = PRESENT\|PROTNONE`, every "CPU can reach it" walker `PRESENT`. The older PRESENT-only `unmap_user`/`protect_user`/`unmap_user_free`/`map_user_anon` family is deleted: the boot self-test `user_mm_tests` now runs on `mm_unmap`/`mm_protect`, so there is one unmap and one protect mechanism. The shared zero frame is allocated once at boot, never freed (recognised by address). `UserSpace::resident` |
| [`kernel/src/syscall/memory.rs`](../kernel/src/syscall/memory.rs) (library, host-tested) | `brk`, `mmap`, `munmap`, `mprotect`, `madvise`; `Context::address_space()` and `Context::mmap_file(fd)` |
| [`kernel/src/kalloc.rs`](../kernel/src/kalloc.rs), [`services.rs`](../kernel/src/services.rs) | The loader's `Vec<Vma>` is built before `FRAMES` exists: `FrameChunks::grow` hands the heap one 64 KiB static boot arena (once) while `Service::is_installed` is false; afterwards chunks are buddy frames as before |
| [`kernel/src/cpu.rs`](../kernel/src/cpu.rs) | `interrupt_handler` returns after `userspace::exception` (a resolved #PF); the existing error-code entry already preserves all fifteen GPRs across a returning #PF (`CHECK page-fault-gprs`) and `iretq`s |
| [`user/init`](../user/init/src/main.rs), [`xtask`](../xtask/src/kernel.rs) | New init mode `-- io_uring` (`io_uring_setup`, which Linux implements and this kernel will not for a long time) for the `unimplemented-syscall` case, which could no longer use `mmap`; new mode `-- demand` (ring-3 demand faults and stack growth, runs unchanged on Linux) for the `demand` case; the `-- mmap` mode and every existing init line are unchanged. New serial records `CHECK mm-usercopy ok ...` required exactly once between `CHECK userspace exec` and init's first line, and `CHECK user-faults ok handled=N` (self-test builds, printed when init exits with status 0) required exactly once between `init: ok` and the death panic |

### Behaviour, with Linux 6.18.57 citations

* **Syscalls.** `mmap` is `arch/x86/kernel/sys_x86_64.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE6(mmap)` (offset must be page aligned, `-EINVAL`) then `mm/mmap.c` `ksys_mmap_pgoff` (`fget` only without `MAP_ANONYMOUS`: `-EBADF`), then the model's `do_mmap` order. Descriptors 0-2 are init's `/dev/console` (`console_fops` in `drivers/tty/tty_io.c` has no `->mmap`, so the model answers `-ENODEV` after the argument checks); anything else is closed (`-EBADF`). `MAP_SHARED|MAP_ANONYMOUS` (shmem), `MAP_HUGETLB` and a file with `->mmap` end in `Outcome::Unimplemented(9)`, never an errno Linux would not return. `brk` (`SYSCALL_DEFINE1(brk)`), `munmap`, `mprotect` return the model's value. `madvise` (`mm/madvise.c`): `is_valid_madvise`-style rejection of behaviours the declared configuration lacks (`CONFIG_KSM`, THP and `MEMORY_FAILURE` are off), unaligned start, `PAGE_ALIGN(len)` wrap, zero length, then the VMA walk (`-ENOMEM` for any hole, after processing the rest); only `MADV_NORMAL` is implemented (it clears `VM_RAND_READ|VM_SEQ_READ`, which no VMA here can have); other valid advice is `Unimplemented(28)`.
* **Page faults.** Ring 3: `exc_page_fault` -> `do_user_addr_fault`: `Handled` returns and the instruction retries; `Segv` is `force_sig_fault(SIGSEGV, SEGV_MAPERR|SEGV_ACCERR, address)` and `Bus` is `do_sigbus` -> `force_sig_fault(SIGBUS, BUS_ADRERR, address)` (`userspace::page_fault`, the model's `FaultOutcome` becomes `info::page_fault`'s siginfo): a handler catches them and the faulting instruction continues where it chooses, otherwise init dies (`show_signal_msg`'s `segfault at ...` line for `SIGSEGV`, printed when the signal is forced, none for `SIGBUS`; exit code 11 / 7); `Oom`, `PgtableBad`, `Vsyscall`, `NeedsFile` are `FAIL mm ...` stops (Linux: OOM killer finding init unkillable, `pgtable_bad` oops, `emulate_vsyscall`, page cache read). Kernel access: `Syscall::page` takes the fault Linux would take for a kernel-mode access to a user address (error code without `X86_PF_USER`): a resolved fault is retried once, a refusal is the exception-table fixup, i.e. a short copy and `-EFAULT` with no signal (`__bad_area_nosemaphore` signals only in user mode). `read(2)` into a fresh anonymous mapping therefore works once the VFS lane adds `read`.
* **Page tables.** Read fault on an absent anonymous page maps the shared zero frame read-only (`do_anonymous_page`); a write fault maps a private zeroed frame, replacing the zero page with `invlpg` (`wp_page_copy`); a write to a present read-only private page of a writable VMA sets the hardware write bit (`wp_page_reuse`); `mprotect` never raises the write bit of a private page (the next write fault does); `PROT_NONE` keeps the frame (`change_protection` + `PAGE_NONE`), so protecting back restores the data (oracle `mprotect-data-preserved`), and, as in Linux, a non-zero not-present entry stores the **complement** of its frame bits (`arch/x86/include/asm/pgtable-invert.h` `__pte_needs_invert`/`protnone_mask`, `pte_pfn`, `pfn_pte`; `pgtable.h:259-281`, `743-776`): an L1TF-affected CPU that speculatively walks the entry reaches a non-existent address, not the page's data. `frame_bits`/`leaf_entry` in `paging.rs` are the only code that touches a leaf's address field. This is the pinned baseline's mitigation, not a delta; the `CHECK mm-usercopy ... protnone=inverted,restored` case reads the raw entry before and after `mprotect(PROT_NONE)` and requires exactly the frame bits complemented, then the original bytes after protecting back (so the un-inversion is exercised on real tables). Not exercised: that an L1TF CPU cannot read the data (no such hardware or speculation test here); PTE inversion for swap/none entries has no counterpart because no such entries exist; `munmap`/`MAP_FIXED` replacement free the data frames after `invlpg` (never the zero frame).
* **Loader.** The first `PT_LOAD` is `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE` (`-EEXIST` against the stack VMA, as in `elf_load`), later ones `MAP_FIXED`, bss through `vm_brk_flags`; the segfault line's `init[off,start+len]` comes from the VMA set after merging, as `print_vma_addr` does.

### Lock rule and contexts

Order `PROCESS` -> `TABLES` -> heap -> `FRAMES` (unchanged). Every `Mm` primitive `try_reserve`s its `Vec` growth before it touches the tables, and `UserSpace::parts` takes `FRAMES` only inside one table operation, so heap growth (which takes `FRAMES`) never runs under `FRAMES`. The #PF handler runs on the kernel stack with interrupts masked, takes `PROCESS` then `TABLES` (ring 3 holds neither), may allocate (fallibly) and returns; it never sleeps. Allocation failure in the model is `ENOMEM` (syscalls) or `Oom` (a loud stop for a fault). `mm_*` helpers panic on a non-exhaustion `Error` (only `NoMemory` is exhaustion; an ownership, alignment or order error is a kernel bug), and `mm_unmap` panics if the allocator refuses a frame it just unmapped, instead of leaking it silently. `PROCESS` and `TABLES` ARE held across a faulting user copy (`Syscall::page` calls `handle_user_fault` under them); that is the same order, with the heap and `FRAMES` below them (`services.rs` states it).

### Evidence

* Host: `cargo +nightly test -p kernel --lib` (kernel library 207+ tests; `exec::load::tests`, `syscall::memory::tests`, the unchanged 37 `mm::` tests plus a loader-installed-page case, and the changed `exec::plan` / `exec::report` tests).
* QEMU: `xtask kernel-test` 26/26 cases (run `mmfix1-gate-001`, logs untracked under `target/kernel-tooling/`), including `init-segfault` (the Linux segfault line from the model's VMA set and `exitcode=0x0000000b`), `unimplemented-syscall` (`-- io_uring`, `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=425`), the new case `demand` (init takes its own ring-3 faults: a read of a fresh anonymous page maps the zero frame, a write replaces it, a write to an untouched page, and a 320 KiB stack frame that grows the stack VMA by ring-3 faults; every access retries transparently and `CHECK user-faults ok handled=N` reports N >= 1, 80 in the recorded run) and the changed record `CHECK mm-usercopy ok fault_in=read,write short_write=4 hole=8 readonly=refused zero_page=shared range=refused protnone=inverted,restored fixed=replaced stack=grown unmap=restored` (the three new fields: the `PROT_NONE` frame inversion and data round trip described above, `MAP_FIXED` over two touched pages leaving demand-zero pages and no leaked frame, and a kernel access one page below the growable stack VMA expanding it by exactly one frame and one page): through the real syscall context, tables and allocator, `mmap`/`mprotect` create the mappings, the user-copy mechanism faults in untouched pages for read (shared zero frame) and write (private frames), stops at a `PROT_NONE` hole with Linux's short counts (4 of 6 bytes; 8 of 8 uncopied and zero-filled at the hole itself), refuses a write to a read-only mapping without copying and keeps the zero page, applies the `access_ok` range check, and `munmap` returns every data frame and `total_vm`.
* Oracle `mm` group (121 checks, `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`): **113 of 121 check lines identical** to the pinned-Linux lines with a stubbed `Probe`, 8 differ, so the stubbed summary line was `passed=113 failed=8` instead of `passed=121 failed=0`. The group cannot run unmodified: `tests/mod.rs` `Probe::new` calls `pipe2`, and `Probe::{readable,writable}` use `pipe2`/`read`/`write` on a pipe, none of which exists before the VFS lane (an unmodified `-- mm` run stops with `FAIL syscall unimplemented nr=293`). **The measurement-only `Probe` stub is not part of the product tree**: its patch, the captured lines and the `diff` against the expected rows are local evidence (untracked, `target/evidence/mm-wiring-measurement/` of the integrating worktree; serial log SHA-256 `f14f8183a5d3543414596215c2769905f40b87f7eca6a043fea0a49147c335a7` of the author's run `fix1-meas`). Nine checks use `Probe`: `mmap-kernel-access`, `prot-none-inaccessible`, `prot-read-only`, `munmap-split-kernel-view`, `mprotect-split-access`, `mprotect-read-only-access`, `mprotect-hole-no-effect`, `mprotect-hole-after-partial`, `mprotect-across-two-vmas-state`; eight of them FAIL with the stub (the answers come from the stub, not the kernel) and `prot-none-inaccessible` passes vacuously. The other 112 checks (all of `brk`, placement, hints, `MAP_FIXED`, `MAP_FIXED_NOREPLACE`, argument errors, `munmap`, `mprotect` return values and data preservation) are the kernel's own answers, byte-identical. The kernel side of the nine is covered by `CHECK mm-usercopy` above (PROT_NONE and read-only refusal, short copies). **The full `mm` oracle case (`oracle_case!(mm)`) landed with the VFS lane ([integration](#vfs-and-memory-mapping-integration): 121/121, identical)**, which provides `pipe2`/`read`/`write` and adds `oracle_case!(mm)` and `oracle_case!(fs)` together; until then `kernel-test` has no `oracle-mm` case and runs only the lane's own non-oracle cases (`demand`, `unimplemented-syscall`) next to `oracle-ids` and `oracle-time`.
* Negative controls for the two `invlpg` sites (QEMU TCG, this tree): removing the `tlb::flush` from `mm_unmap` and, separately, from `mm_protect` makes the boot self-test fail (`FAIL user-mm error=Conflict`, 19 of 26 cases failed in each run: the self-test runs before ring 3 in every userspace case); logs `fix1-neg-unmap` and `fix1-neg-protect` (untracked). Evidence for TCG's TLB model on this cell, not for real hardware.
* Gates: `target/gates.sh` run `mmfix1-gate-001` on the author's workstation (same host, user and toolchain installation as earlier records; not independently attested), final tree of branch `feat/mm-syscalls` after review fix round 1: `cargo fmt --all --check`, host 1.88 Clippy and tests of `xtask`/`baremetal` (76 xtask tests), nightly workspace Clippy (`-D warnings`) and tests (kernel library **207** passed), `xtask kernel-test` **26/26** cases matched, both target Clippy profiles (`qemu-self-test`, `freestanding`), `xtask check` and `xtask status` (18 `in_progress`, 2,605 `not_started`) all `exit=0` (logs `target/gates-mmfix1/`, untracked). Earlier runs `mmw-gate-001`/`-002` failed Clippy (doc backticks, a `u32 as i32` cast, test helper signatures, an unused `mut`, no-op `drop`s, a 125-line function) and `mmw-gate-003` passed before the review; they were fixed and re-run in full. During fix round 1 a first `kernel-test` run failed 13/25 because the new `user-faults` record was required to be non-zero for every case (the loader populates init's pages, so only an init that faults itself takes ring-3 faults); the requirement moved to the new `demand` case.
* Integration onto `main` (after PRs #19 identity and #20 time; routing now 21 `in_progress`, 2,602 `not_started`): rebased with conflicts only in `syscall/mod.rs` (`Context` is the union: `Clocks` supertrait, identity methods, `address_space`, `mmap_file`), `syscall/testing.rs`, `userspace.rs` (`Process` keeps `attrs`, `load`, `restart` and gains `space`/`env`; the `vmas`/`brk` fields are gone), `xtask/src/kernel.rs` (`DEBUG_CASES` stays a slice with `oracle_case!` lines for `ids`/`time`; the lane's `demand` case kept) and the documentation. Full gates `target/gates.sh` runs `mm-int-1` (all `exit=0` except `fmt`, one line removed by rustfmt) and `mm-int-2` on the rebased tree; `oracle-mm` is absent by design (it needs the VFS lane's `pipe2`/`read`/`write`).
* Not exercised: Vec `try_reserve` failure under QEMU, frame exhaustion in a user fault, SMP/TLB shootdown, `MAP_POPULATE` of file pages, any file mapping.

### Not done (named, not hidden)

File-backed mappings beyond private tmpfs files (done by the VFS lane, see [the integration](#vfs-and-memory-mapping-integration); writable `MAP_SHARED` file mappings and a real page cache remain), shmem and hugetlb, `mremap`, `mlock*`, `mincore`, `fork`/COW sharing, `process_vm_*`, `/proc/self/maps` and `smaps`, RSS accounting counters (`MM_ANONPAGES`), `RLIMIT_*` updates through `setrlimit` (the fixed `INIT_RLIMITS` in `Env`), signal delivery for faults (SIGSEGV/SIGBUS are today's direct init death), the vsyscall emulation, VMA count growth beyond the boot arena without frames (cannot happen: frames exist from the end of boot), the O(n) sorted-vector VMA set.

### The five design questions

1. *Crate?* None new. The mapping rules are the project's own model; the heap is the already vendored `buddy_system_allocator` over a static arena; page-table access reuses the existing `Tables` walkers.
2. *Bundle?* Yes: one `Mm` for the loader, five syscalls and both fault paths; one `UserPages` implementation per table owner behind a private `Backing` trait (runtime vs loader allocator) instead of two copies; one usercopy (`Syscall::page` is the only kernel-access fault point).
3. *Simplify?* No `vm_operations_struct`, rmap or page cache: the loader installs file pages eagerly as private frames; `madvise` implements the generic checks plus `MADV_NORMAL` only. Not simplified: errno order, `max_map_count` strictness, merge/split visible through the segfault line, short-copy counts.
4. *Secure?* Every user address goes through the range check and the fault-aware resolver; no user pointer is dereferenced. A user can no longer stop the kernel through `mmap`/`munmap`/`mprotect`/`brk`/`madvise` except the explicit shmem/hugetlb/`madvise`-advice `Unimplemented` stops (documented single-init shortcut). Mapped means `MAPPED` and reachable means `PRESENT`, so a `PROT_NONE` page can never be read by the CPU or by usercopy, and its stored frame is complemented as in Linux (L1TF); the zero frame is never writable (checked by address in `mm_make_writable`/`mm_unmap`). Frame ownership: every user leaf owns exactly one order-zero reservation except the zero frame. Heap and frames are fallible; exhaustion is `ENOMEM`/`Oom`, never a panic on user input. Risks left: no SMP shootdown, no `RLIMIT_AS` update path.
5. *Improve?* Add the VFS file mapping path and replace eager installation with a page cache; (`page_fault` now forces the signal, see [Signal wiring](#signal-wiring-integration-with-the-merged-lanes)); `RSS` counters and `/proc/self/maps`; an augmented tree when `map_count` matters.

## VFS spine

Roadmap slice WP-M3.9 (first part): the kernel now has a root filesystem. `/init` is no longer found by walking the boot archives; every boot module is unpacked by a state-for-state translation of `init/initramfs.c` into a **tmpfs rootfs**, and `/init` and the fallback inits are opened from it with `kernel_execve`'s flags. The file syscalls the oracle `fs` group uses run on that tree. Pure `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` library code in `kernel/src/vfs/` and `kernel/src/syscall/{fd,fs}.rs` with 30 + 16 host tests; the binary only builds the rootfs (`userspace.rs`) and hands the `Vfs` to the one `Process`. It is a bounded single-task, single-mount cell, not Linux's VFS.

### What exists

| File | Linux source (pinned 6.18.57) | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `vfs/tmpfs.rs` | `mm/shmem.c` (`__shmem_get_inode`, `shmem_mknod/mkdir/link/unlink/rmdir/symlink`, `shmem_setattr/getattr`, `shmem_file_read_iter`, `shmem_reserve_inode`, `shmem_inode_acct_blocks`, `BOGO_DIRENT_SIZE`), `fs/libfs.c` (`simple_offset_add`, `offset_readdir`, `DIR_OFFSET_*`), `fs/inode.c` (`inode_init_owner`) | Inode arena (`u32` indices), directory entries with Linux's offsets (listed newest first, `d_off` = offset of the next entry, `DIR_OFFSET_EOD`), sparse sorted page store, block and inode limits (`ENOSPC`), reference counts that keep an unlinked inode alive while open or while a working directory |
| `vfs/path.rs` | `fs/namei.c` (`link_path_walk`, `walk_component`, `step_into`/`pick_link`, `may_lookup`, `path_init`, `filename_parentat`, `acl_permission_check`/`generic_permission`) | Component walk with `.`/`..`, root clamp, trailing slash, `MAXSYMLINKS` 40, `NAME_MAX`, directory and execute-bit rules, parent-for-create; the permission check uses `Cred` (root bypass needs an execute bit somewhere to execute a regular file) |
| `vfs/ops.rs` | `fs/open.c` (`build_open_flags`, `do_dentry_open`, `chdir`, `do_faccessat`), `fs/namei.c` (`do_filp_open`, `open_last_lookups`, `do_open`, `may_open`, `lookup_open`, `vfs_mknod/mkdir/symlink/link/unlink/rmdir`, `may_delete`, sticky rule), `fs/char_dev.c` (`chrdev_open`), `fs/fs_struct.c` (`set_fs_pwd`), `fs/d_path.c` (`prepend_path`), `init/main.c` (`console_on_rootfs`), `fs/pipe.c` (`create_pipe_files`) | `Vfs`: `lookup`, `open` with Linux's flag/error order (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL`, `O_TRUNC`, `O_DIRECTORY`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, `O_PATH`, `O_TMPFILE`, `ENXIO` for character major 0 and `/dev/tty` without a controlling terminal; any other character or block number opens as an unported device whose use stops loudly), creation and removal, `dir_path` (the `getcwd` text, `ENOENT` for a removed cwd), `console_on_rootfs` (fds 0-2 are one `/dev/console` file opened `O_RDWR` without `O_LARGEFILE`), pipe files |
| `vfs/file.rs` | `fs/file.c` (`alloc_fd`, `expand_files`, `do_dup2`, `file_close_fd_locked`, `sysctl_nr_open`), `fs/file_table.c`, `include/linux/fs.h` (`FMODE_*`), `fs/fs_struct.c` | `FileTable` (open file descriptions, shared by `dup`), `FdTable` (lowest-free allocation from a `next_fd` hint, open and close-on-exec bitmaps on the shared `kernel::bitmap`, power-of-two growth, `EMFILE`), `FsStruct` (root, cwd, umask: `task::Attributes::fs`) |
| `vfs/pipe.rs` | `fs/pipe.c` (`anon_pipe_read`, `anon_pipe_write` merge and page rules, `PIPE_DEF_BUFFERS`) | Sixteen page buffers, merge into the newest buffer, `FIONREAD`/`F_GETPIPE_SZ`, reader and writer counts |
| `vfs/initramfs.rs` | `init/initramfs.c` (`unpack_to_rootfs`, the `do_*` state machine, `maybe_link`, `clean_path`, `dir_utime`, `xwrite`), `init/noinitramfs.c` / `usr/default_cpio_list` | `Unpacker`: archive after archive with `next_state` carried over, NUL padding between archives, entries kept up to the first error, Linux's messages (`no cpio magic`, `incorrect cpio method used: use -H newc option`, `malformed archive`, `bad data checksum`, `write error`, a compressed module is reported with the non-Linux `NOT_PORTED` text, since no decompressor is ported, and a boot that then finds no `/init` stops with `FAIL initramfs module=<n> is compressed ...` instead of imitating Linux's `NO_INIT`), the builtin `/dev`, `/dev/console` (5:1, 0600) and `/root` (0700) |
| `syscall/fd.rs` | `fs/read_write.c`, `lib/iov_iter.c` (`__import_iovec`), `fs/file.c`, `fs/fcntl.c`, `fs/ioctl.c`, `fs/readdir.c`, `fs/pipe.c`, `drivers/char/mem.c`, `drivers/tty` (`TCGETS`, `TIOCGWINSZ`, `TIOCGPGRP`), `fs/open.c` (`do_sys_ftruncate`) | `read` `write` `pread64` `pwrite64` `readv` `preadv` `lseek` `close` `dup` `dup2` `dup3` `fcntl` `ioctl` `pipe2` `getdents64` `ftruncate` with `vfs_read`/`vfs_write` check order (mode, `access_ok`, `rw_verify_area`, `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp), short copies, page-sized copy granularity, `O_APPEND`, no-`O_LARGEFILE` limits |
| `syscall/fs.rs` | `fs/open.c`, `fs/namei.c` (`getname_flags`, `do_mknodat`, `do_unlinkat`), `fs/stat.c` (`cp_new_stat`, `cp_statx`, `vfs_fstatat`, `do_statx`), `fs/d_path.c` (`getcwd`), `lib/strncpy_from_user.c` | `open` `openat` `mknod` `unlink` `access` `faccessat` `faccessat2` `chdir` `fchdir` `getcwd` `readlink` `readlinkat` `stat` `lstat` `newfstatat` `statx`; pathnames are copied once (`strncpy_from_user`, `ENAMETOOLONG`, `AT_EMPTY_PATH`), then the `Vfs` never sees user memory |
| `userspace.rs` | `init/main.c` (`kernel_init_freeable`, `kernel_init`: `init_eaccess`, the fallback list), `fs/exec.c` (`do_open_execat`: regular file, execute bit), `init/do_mounts.c` (failure texts, kept) | `build_rootfs`, `/init` and fallbacks looked up in the rootfs, `open_exec` reading the whole file (a temporary copy; `shortcut` noted), `Process` owning `Vfs` + `FdTable`, timekeeping start stamping the unpack-time placeholders |

Context and ownership: task context only; nothing sleeps. An operation that Linux would sleep in (an empty pipe with a writer, a full pipe, console input) has no other task or interrupt source to wake it, so it ends in the new loud `Outcome::Blocked(nr)`, the kernel prints `FAIL syscall would block forever nr=<n>` and stops; `O_NONBLOCK` returns `EAGAIN` as Linux does. Writing to a pipe without readers sends `SIGPIPE` (`send_sig`, `SI_USER` from this process) and returns `EPIPE` (the bytes already written when the last reader went away are returned instead), a write or `ftruncate` growth past `RLIMIT_FSIZE` sends `SIGXFSZ` and returns `EFBIG`. The sleeps are interruptible now that signals exist: an empty pipe or console read and a full pipe write wait with `wait_event_interruptible` (`fd::interruptible_wait`), and a signal that is pending (the only wake-up source is an armed `ITIMER_REAL`) ends the call with `-ERESTARTSYS` (the partial count for a partial write), which the return to user mode makes `EINTR` or a restart; a wait nothing could ever end (no signal pending, no timer armed) is still the loud `Outcome::Blocked`. Every allocation is fallible (`try_reserve` + `push_within_capacity`; `ENOMEM`/`ENOSPC`), and no heap allocation happens while `FRAMES` is held (`load_init` takes the frames after the executable is read). Lock order for a second task: `PROCESS` -> `vfs` (a new class, one per superblock) -> `TABLES` -> heap -> `FRAMES`; with one task and one CPU the caller's exclusive borrow of `Vfs` is the whole synchronisation story (Linux's `i_rwsem`, `d_lock`, `file_lock`, `f_pos_lock` collapse into it). The rootfs is built before CR3 publication because the boot modules are only readable then; its timestamps carry a placeholder until `start` stamps them with `ktime_get_real` (`timer.rs`, the timekeeping core), exactly as files created at boot carry the boot wall clock.

Integration seams with the other lanes: `task::Attributes::fs` is `vfs::FsStruct` (the ids lane's `umask(2)` edits `fs.umask`; its old `getcwd` over a fixed path is replaced by `Vfs::dir_path`); `RLIMIT_NOFILE` and the credentials come from `Attributes` (`Context::rlimit_nofile`, `Context::cred`; `Cred::of_task` maps euid 0 to every capability the VFS asks for, a `shortcut` until the credentials work lands); file-backed `mmap` needs only `Tmpfs::read(inode, pos, buf)` and the inode identity, which the memory-mapping lane calls from its `NeedsFile` outcome; `SIGPIPE` is the single `Stop::SigPipe` site in `syscall/fd.rs`.

### Not done (named, not hidden)

`mkdir`/`rmdir`/`rename`/`link`/`symlink`/`chmod`/`chown`/`utime*` syscalls (the `Vfs` operations exist for the unpacker and are tested), `mount`/`umount`/`pivot_root`/`chroot`, `fsync`/`sync`, `flock`/`fcntl` locks, `fasync`/`SIGIO`, `F_SETPIPE_SZ`, `splice`/`tee`/`sendfile`, `O_DIRECT`, `O_TMPFILE` data path beyond creation, xattrs and ACLs, `devtmpfs`, procfs/sysfs, FIFOs and sockets (opening one stops loudly), the page cache as a separate layer (tmpfs pages are the data), `atime` updates on symlink traversal and pipe reads (reads, `getdents64` and `readlink` update `atime` on every call: the rootfs is mounted without `MNT_RELATIME`, `fs/namespace.c:6110`), `SIGXFSZ` delivery (a write or `ftruncate` past `RLIMIT_FSIZE` is a loud stop), `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`/`FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR`/`FS_IOC_FIEMAP`/`FICLONE*`/`FIDEDUPERANGE`/`FS_IOC_GETFSUUID`/`FS_IOC_GETFSSYSFSPATH`/`FIBMAP`/`FS_IOC_RESVSP*` (loud stops), the drivers behind every character and block number other than `null`/`zero`/`full`/console (open succeeds, use stops), RCU path walk and the dentry cache (a directory is a vector scanned linearly; a hash index is the upgrade), decompression of the initramfs, `mmap` of files and of `/dev/zero`, supplementary groups and real capabilities. Each loud stop is a `Stop::Unimplemented` at the place Linux would do something this slice cannot.

### The five design questions

1. *Crate?* None. cpio crates (`cpio`, `cpio_reader`) parse but do not reproduce `init/initramfs.c`'s state machine, its partial-failure tree or its ignore-and-continue rules; `image::cpio::hex_field` (already Linux's `simple_strntoul`) is reused. A VFS/tmpfs crate with Linux semantics does not exist; `ramfs`-style crates fail `no_global_oom_handling` or the exact `d_off`, size and errno rules the oracle records.
2. *Bundle?* One `Vfs` owns tmpfs, files and pipes, one path walker serves `open`, the stat family, `chdir`, the unpacker and the loader, one `FdTable` serves every descriptor syscall, one user-copy mechanism (`usercopy`) sits under all of them, and the console write path stays the single `io::tty_write` that `write` reaches through the file kind. The old `exec::find` archive walk is deleted rather than kept beside the unpacker.
3. *Simplify?* A dentry and an inode are one arena entry; the dentry cache, mounts and the superblock list collapse into one in-memory instance; `struct file_operations` is the `FileKind` enum dispatched by the syscall layer; locks collapse into the caller's exclusive borrow. Not simplified: error order, `d_off` cookies, directory sizes, `O_APPEND`/`O_LARGEFILE` limits, the permission algorithm, partial-copy results and the unpacker's partial-failure behaviour, because each is observable.
4. *Secure?* No `unsafe` in any new file. Every user pointer goes through `usercopy`; pathnames and iovecs are copied before use; sizes are bounded (`PATH_MAX`, `NAME_MAX`, `UIO_MAXIOV`, `MAX_RW_COUNT`, `NR_OPEN`); the archive is untrusted and parsed with checked arithmetic, bounded name and symlink lengths and Linux's own rejection of malformed headers (a hostile name cannot escape the tree: it is resolved as an ordinary path, `..` clamps at the root); tmpfs is bounded by the block and inode limits; handles are arena indices kept valid by counts, and a stale handle panics (a kernel invariant, not reachable from user input because descriptors and working directories hold counts). Remaining risks: a user can fill the rootfs up to half of RAM (as on Linux); `shortcut` linear directory scans are quadratic for a directory with very many names.
5. *Improve?* A hash index per directory; reading `/init` through page references instead of a temporary copy; sharing tmpfs pages with the page cache and file-backed `mmap`; a decompressor for compressed initramfs images; a second task needs the `vfs` lock class and real `files_struct` sharing.

### Evidence

Host tests: `cargo +nightly test -p kernel --lib vfs::` (32 tests: default rootfs, sparse writes and holes, truncate, block and inode limits, `d_off` cookies and resume, directory sizes and link counts, removal error precedence, hard links and unlinked-open files, walk rules including loops and long names, permission classes and the sticky root, umask, `open` flag order, device selection, the descriptor table limits and `EBUSY`, shared files, pipes, the unpacker with attributes, hard links, replacement by type, concatenated and truncated archives, damaged-archive messages, a full rootfs, replacement of `/init` by later modules, extra hard links charged as inodes, a dead directory) and `syscall::vfs_tests` (20 tests through `dispatch`: read/write/seek/stat, argument-error order, append, `dup*`/`fcntl`, `RLIMIT_NOFILE`, pipes and the loud stops, `pipe2` flags, memory devices, console, directories with `chdir`/`getdents64`/`getcwd`, path errors, unlink with open files, the stat family, `access`/`readlink`/`ftruncate`, vectored I/O, unported behaviour).

(Superseded by [the integration onto main](#vfs-and-memory-mapping-integration): `oracle-fs` now gates all 284 checks, and the interim case, `User::OracleSkip` and the `fs-nommap` mode are removed.) QEMU: the oracle `fs` group (`user/init/src/tests/fs.rs`, 284 checks recorded on the pinned Linux 6.18.57 in `xtask/oracle/expected.tsv`) runs against this kernel through `kernel-test` case `oracle-fs-no-mmap` (`-- fs-nommap`, `User::OracleSkip`): the `/init` mode `fs-nommap` skips exactly the **14 checks that call `mmap`** (the 13 `fs.mmap-*` checks and `fs.dev-zero-mmap-private`), and the validator requires every other expected line (270 checks and the one `info` row, whose `d_off` cookies equal the recording in value: `[1, 7, 6, 5, 4, 3, 2147483647]`) in order, with the summary rebuilt as `init: tests fs passed=270 failed=0`; a skipped check that ran, a missing or failing check or a stale count each fail the case (`skipping_oracle_case_expects_every_other_check_and_a_rebuilt_summary`). This replaces the earlier `oracle-fs-until-mmap`, which stopped at the first `mmap` and left 25 checks (`dev-*`, `mknod-*`, `root-home-directory`, ...) ungated. When `mmap` lands, replace the case by `oracle_case!(fs)` and delete the `fs-nommap` mode. Existing cases are unchanged and pass with `/init` loaded from the rootfs (`success`, `no-init` with Linux's `prepare_namespace` text, `bad-module`, `empty-module`, the segfault and `unimplemented-syscall` cases).

### Review round 1

Independent review of the first VFS spine found one blocking and eleven should-fix defects; each is fixed in a focused commit (`kernel: VFS review round 1 ...`, `oracle: gate the 270 non-mmap fs checks ...`) with host tests (`syscall::vfs_tests`, `vfs::tests`). No finding was rejected.

| `FIOQSIZE` returned `i_size` | `inode_get_bytes` = `blocks() << 9` (0 for a directory, 4096 for a 5-byte file) | `fs/ioctl.c:513-522`, `fs/stat.c:960` | `vfs_ioctls_answer_as_tmpfs_does` |
| `do_vfs_ioctl` commands answered `ENOTTY` | `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS`/`FS_IOC_FSGETXATTR` succeed with flags 0 on regular files and directories (`shmem_fileattr_get`, `CONFIG_TMPFS_XATTR=y`); `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`, `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR`, `FS_IOC_FIEMAP`, `FICLONE*`, `FIDEDUPERANGE`, `FS_IOC_GETFSUUID`, `FS_IOC_GETFSSYSFSPATH`, and on regular files `FIBMAP`/`FS_IOC_RESVSP*`/`FS_IOC_ZERO_RANGE` stop loudly; `FIGETBSZ` is 4096 for every file including a pipe | `fs/ioctl.c:530-571`, `mm/shmem.c:4240,5279,5301`, `fs/file_attr.c` | same |
